- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Large AI Model-Enabled Secure Communications in Low-Altitude Wireless Networks: Concepts, Perspectives and Case Study
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00256v1)
- **Authors**: Chuang Zhang, Geng Sun, Jiacheng Wang, Yijing Lin, Weijie Yuan, Sinem Coleri, Dusit Niyato, Tony Q. S. Quek
- **Abstract**: Low-altitude wireless networks (LAWNs) have the potential to revolutionize communications by supporting a range of applications, including urban parcel delivery, aerial inspections and air taxis. However, compared with traditional wireless networks, LAWNs face unique security challenges due to low-altitude operations, frequent mobility and reliance on unlicensed spectrum, making it more vulnerable to some malicious attacks. In this paper, we investigate some large artificial intelligence model (LAM)-enabled solutions for secure communications in LAWNs. Specifically, we first explore the amplified security risks and important limitations of traditional AI methods in LAWNs. Then, we introduce the basic concepts of LAMs and delve into the role of LAMs in addressing these challenges. To demonstrate the practical benefits of LAMs for secure communications in LAWNs, we propose a novel LAM-based optimization framework that leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate enhanced state features on top of handcrafted representations, and to design intrinsic rewards accordingly, thereby improving reinforcement learning performance for secure communication tasks. Through a typical case study, simulation results validate the effectiveness of the proposed framework. Finally, we outline future directions for integrating LAMs into secure LAWN applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the application of large AI models (LAMs) to improve secure communications in low-altitude wireless networks (LAWNs).  It argues that traditional AI methods have limitations in the complex and dynamic environment of LAWNs (due to low altitude operations, mobility, and unlicensed spectrum usage), making them vulnerable to attacks.  The paper introduces the concept of using LAMs to address these challenges by providing enhanced capabilities for generalization, adaptation to dynamic environments, and integration of multi-modal data.  It proposes a novel LAM-based optimization framework that uses large language models (LLMs) to generate improved state features and design intrinsic rewards, thus boosting reinforcement learning performance for security tasks.  Simulation results from a case study validate the proposed framework. Finally, the paper discusses future research directions for LAM integration in secure LAWN applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of applying LAMs to enhance security in LAWNs has some novelty. While traditional AI has been used, LAMs bring capabilities like multi-modal integration, proactive reasoning, and better generalization that could address limitations. The proposed LLM-enhanced optimization framework also provides a somewhat innovative approach. However, the paper heavily relies on general concepts of LAMs and lacks specific technical innovation.
* **Significance:** If successful, the ideas presented could significantly impact the security of LAWNs, which are becoming increasingly important for various applications. Addressing the security challenges related to LAWNs, like detection, jamming, eavesdropping, and tampering, is critical for their safe and reliable operation. The paper highlights a key area of concern.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-structured and organized:** The paper is clearly written and logically organized.
    * **Comprehensive overview:** The paper gives a thorough overview of LAWN security challenges, traditional AI limitations, and LAM capabilities.
    * **Identifies relevant research directions:** The paper outlines important areas for future research, such as domain-specific datasets and efficient LAM deployment on resource-constrained platforms.
    * **Validation with Simulation:** A limited simulation provides some evidence of the feasibility of the proposed ideas.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Lacks technical depth:**  The paper is very conceptual and does not delve into the specifics of the LAM architecture or its implementation. How to actually fine-tune the LLM is glossed over with generic advice, and real-world implementation details are absent.
    * **Limited simulation results:** The case study and simulations are basic and lack the complexity required to truly evaluate the effectiveness of the framework in real-world LAWN scenarios. Details regarding dataset construction for training are not provided.
    * **Lack of Specificity:** The paper discusses general approaches without really specifying concrete solutions for each of the identified security challenges.
    * **Overstatement of Capabilities:** The paper frequently uses phrases like "LAMs excel in," "LAMs provide robust," which sounds promotional without specific evidence. There needs to be a balanced assessment of the challenges that may arise when applying LAMs to the domain.
    * **Missing Baselines:** The chosen baselines are rather simplistic. A more comprehensive evaluation would include comparisons against more sophisticated (albeit, non-LAM) approaches for security in wireless networks.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper can serve as a good starting point for future research on applying LAMs to secure LAWNs. It could encourage researchers to explore this promising area and develop more concrete solutions. However, its influence will depend on subsequent work that builds upon the ideas presented and addresses the limitations.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents an interesting application of LAMs to an important area of security in LAWNs, and offers a framework. However, the lack of technical detail, the limited simulation results, and conceptual nature of the paper significantly reduce its overall impact. The provided results serve more as a demonstration of the feasibility of the general idea rather than a comprehensive evaluation, therefore I am assigning a modest score.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 5/10

### Instruction-Grounded Visual Projectors for Continual Learning of Generative Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00260v1)
- **Authors**: Hyundong Jin, Hyung Jin Chang, Eunwoo Kim
- **Abstract**: Continual learning enables pre-trained generative vision-language models (VLMs) to incorporate knowledge from new tasks without retraining data from previous ones. Recent methods update a visual projector to translate visual information for new tasks, connecting pre-trained vision encoders with large language models. However, such adjustments may cause the models to prioritize visual inputs over language instructions, particularly learning tasks with repetitive types of textual instructions. To address the neglect of language instructions, we propose a novel framework that grounds the translation of visual information on instructions for language models. We introduce a mixture of visual projectors, each serving as a specialized visual-to-language translation expert based on the given instruction context to adapt to new tasks. To avoid using experts for irrelevant instruction contexts, we propose an expert recommendation strategy that reuses experts for tasks similar to those previously learned. Additionally, we introduce expert pruning to alleviate interference from the use of experts that cumulatively activated in previous tasks. Extensive experiments on diverse vision-language tasks demonstrate that our method outperforms existing continual learning approaches by generating instruction-following responses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of continual learning in generative Vision-Language Models (VLMs). It observes that existing methods, which update the visual projector of VLMs for new tasks, can lead to the neglect of language instructions, especially when instructions are repetitive. The paper proposes a novel framework called "Mixture of Visual Projectors" (MVP) that grounds visual information translation on language instructions. MVP introduces a mixture of visual projectors, each acting as a specialist for different instruction contexts. To encourage the use of appropriate experts, it uses an expert recommendation strategy based on semantic similarity between tasks and employs expert pruning to avoid negative transfer. The framework adaptively aggregates visual information from the mixture of projectors and the pre-trained projector to preserve zero-shot capabilities. Extensive experiments on diverse vision-language tasks demonstrate that MVP outperforms existing continual learning approaches by generating instruction-following responses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components:

    *   The core idea of using a mixture of visual projectors conditioned on language instructions is a good concept.
    *   The expert recommendation strategy, based on semantic relevance, is a useful mechanism to avoid catastrophic forgetting and promote knowledge transfer.
    *   Expert pruning is a valuable addition for mitigating negative transfer.
    *   The adaptive knowledge aggregation strategy is designed to retain the zero-shot performance of pre-trained VLMs.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in addressing a key limitation of continual learning in VLMs: the neglect of language instructions. By grounding visual information translation on language instructions, MVP allows VLMs to adapt to new tasks without sacrificing the ability to follow instructions or the ability to generalize in zero-shot settings. The method offers potential for improving the adaptability and robustness of VLMs in dynamic and ever-changing environments.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and explains the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Well-Designed Framework:** The proposed framework is well-structured and modular, with each component addressing a specific challenge.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are extensive and evaluate the method on a diverse set of vision-language tasks. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   **Performance:** The method consistently outperforms existing continual learning methods, demonstrating its effectiveness.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The framework is relatively complex, with multiple components and hyperparameters. The practical implementation and tuning could be challenging.
    *   **Generality:** The success of the method relies on the ability to define and categorize instruction contexts effectively. In scenarios with extremely diverse and complex language instructions, the performance might be limited. The specific language instructions utilized are simple. More research on the effect of more sophisticated, fine-grained language instructions is needed.
    *   **Limited analysis of Expert Composition.** It is beneficial to analyze which experts are activated and/or pruned. Does this reflect the ground truth nature of each sequential learning task?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Inspiring new approaches for continual learning in VLMs that explicitly consider language instructions.
    *   Providing a foundation for developing more adaptable and robust VLMs.
    *   Motivating further research into methods for grounding visual information on language.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a critical problem in continual learning of VLMs. The improvements demonstrated through experiments are significant. However, the complexity of the framework and potential limitations in handling extremely diverse instructions prevent it from achieving a higher score. A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contribution and potential impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Calibrated Language Models and How to Find Them with Label Smoothing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00264v1)
- **Authors**: Jerry Huang, Peng Lu, Qiuhao Zeng
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in natural language processing (NLP) have opened up greater opportunities to enable fine-tuned large language models (LLMs) to behave as more powerful interactive agents through improved instruction-following ability. However, understanding how this impacts confidence calibration for reliable model output has not been researched in full. In this work, we examine various open-sourced LLMs, identifying significant calibration degradation after instruction tuning in each. Seeking a practical solution, we look towards label smoothing, which has been shown as an effective method to regularize for overconfident predictions but has yet to be widely adopted in the supervised fine-tuning (SFT) of LLMs. We first provide insight as to why label smoothing is sufficient to maintain calibration throughout the SFT process. However, settings remain where the effectiveness of smoothing is severely diminished, in particular the case of large vocabulary LLMs (LV-LLMs). We posit the cause to stem from the ability to become over-confident, which has a direct relationship with the hidden size and vocabulary size, and justify this theoretically and experimentally. Finally, we address an outstanding issue regarding the memory footprint of the cross-entropy loss computation in the label smoothed loss setting, designing a customized kernel to dramatically reduce memory consumption without sacrificing speed or performance in comparison to existing solutions for non-smoothed losses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the issue of calibration degradation in large language models (LLMs) after instruction fine-tuning (SFT). It investigates the use of label smoothing (LS) as a method to address this issue, showing that while it can be effective, its performance diminishes with large vocabulary LLMs (LV-LLMs). The paper provides a theoretical explanation based on a relationship between model entropy, hidden size, and vocabulary size. It also addresses the memory footprint issue associated with LS by proposing a custom kernel that reduces memory consumption during cross-entropy loss computation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has several contributions, some more novel than others.
    *   The observation that SFT degrades calibration is not entirely new, but its systematic demonstration across various LLMs is valuable.
    *   The analysis of why LS fails with LV-LLMs, linking it to model size and entropy, is a more significant contribution, providing a theoretical grounding for observed behavior.
    *   The custom kernel for memory-efficient LS is a practical contribution, although memory efficient kernels for common operations are a well-trodden field.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper addresses a relevant and important problem: the reliability of LLM outputs. Poor calibration hinders the trustworthy use of these models in real-world applications.
    *   Providing practical solutions for implementing label smoothing with large vocabularies addresses a crucial issue. However, the analysis and solution might be limited to LLMs and not broadly applicable to other domains.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides ample empirical evidence to support its claims, testing across several LLM architectures and datasets.
    *   **Theoretical Explanation:** The paper's theoretical analysis attempts to explain the observed phenomena, providing a deeper understanding of the underlying mechanisms.
    *   **Practical Contribution:** The custom kernel is a concrete solution to a practical problem.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental:** While solid, several contributions are incremental improvements upon existing work, such as the kernel optimization. The underlying principle of using memory efficient kernels is well understood.
    *   **Limited Generality:** The findings are primarily focused on LLMs.
    *   **Missing Baselines:** The custom kernel needs to be evaluated against more recent memory efficient kernel operations.
    *   **Theoretical Model Simplifications:** The assumptions made in the theoretical model may not perfectly capture the complexity of real LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper could influence how LLMs are fine-tuned, encouraging the use of LS and other calibration techniques.
    *   The memory-efficient kernel could be adopted by practitioners struggling with memory limitations.
    *   The theoretical analysis could inspire further research into the relationship between model size, entropy, and calibration.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper addresses a relevant problem and offers both theoretical insights and practical solutions. However, the novelty of individual contributions, particularly the kernel optimization, is somewhat incremental. The results and analysis is well done, with some weaknesses described above.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multimodal Referring Segmentation: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00265v1)
- **Authors**: Henghui Ding, Song Tang, Shuting He, Chang Liu, Zuxuan Wu, Yu-Gang Jiang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal referring segmentation aims to segment target objects in visual scenes, such as images, videos, and 3D scenes, based on referring expressions in text or audio format. This task plays a crucial role in practical applications requiring accurate object perception based on user instructions. Over the past decade, it has gained significant attention in the multimodal community, driven by advances in convolutional neural networks, transformers, and large language models, all of which have substantially improved multimodal perception capabilities. This paper provides a comprehensive survey of multimodal referring segmentation. We begin by introducing this field's background, including problem definitions and commonly used datasets. Next, we summarize a unified meta architecture for referring segmentation and review representative methods across three primary visual scenes, including images, videos, and 3D scenes. We further discuss Generalized Referring Expression (GREx) methods to address the challenges of real-world complexity, along with related tasks and practical applications. Extensive performance comparisons on standard benchmarks are also provided. We continually track related works at https://github.com/henghuiding/Awesome-Multimodal-Referring-Segmentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided survey paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive survey of multimodal referring segmentation, a task aiming to segment objects in visual scenes based on referring expressions (text or audio). It covers background information, a unified meta-architecture, representative methods across image, video (including audio-visual), and 3D scenes.  The survey also discusses generalized referring expressions (GREx), related tasks, applications, and performance comparisons. A GitHub repository is maintained for continuously tracking related works.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty and Significance:** The primary contribution is providing a structured and unified overview of a rapidly evolving field.  While surveys inherently involve summarizing existing work, this paper demonstrates significance by:
    *   *Comprehensiveness:* The survey tackles a broad range of modalities and scene types (image, video, 3D), which is more extensive than most existing surveys that often focus on a single modality or task. This broad scope makes it highly valuable for researchers new to the field and those seeking connections between different sub-areas.
    *   *Unified Meta-Architecture:* The proposed meta-architecture offers a useful framework for understanding and comparing various approaches. This helps to abstract away task-specific details and highlight common underlying principles.
    *   *GREx and Real-world Relevance:*  The inclusion of GREx and the focus on related applications demonstrates an awareness of real-world complexity and the need for generalization beyond standard benchmark datasets.
    *   *Well-Organized Structure:* The survey is well-structured, making it easy to navigate and find specific information.

* **Strengths:**
    *   Detailed and up-to-date literature review with over 600 papers covered.
    *   Clear taxonomy and task definitions.
    *   Well-structured and easy-to-follow presentation.
    *   The maintenance of a GitHub repository is a significant asset, providing ongoing value beyond the initial publication date.
    *   Inclusion of recent trends like foundation models and reasoning segmentation.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Depth of Analysis:*  While comprehensive, the survey could benefit from more critical analysis of the strengths and weaknesses of different methods, and from a deeper comparison of architectural choices.  The paper mainly summarizes the approaches, which, although useful, is not as insightful as a more comparative deep dive.
    *   *Bias towards deep learning:* It predominantly focuses on deep learning methods, which is understandable given the current landscape, but could give less attention to other potentially relevant techniques or historical perspectives.
    *   *Limited discussion of future trends/challenges:* It is important for surveys to not only document the present but also look forward.  While it includes some discussion of future directions, it could be further expanded to explicitly discuss potential challenges in each sub-area.
    *   *Benchmark Results Analysis:*  The paper provides benchmark tables, but the associated discussion focuses primarily on which approaches perform the best, without necessarily analysing why these approaches are stronger.

* **Potential Influence:** The survey can serve as a valuable resource for researchers, helping them to quickly grasp the state of the art, identify promising research directions, and compare existing methods.  It also offers a useful starting point for newcomers to the field. However, its impact is limited by the weaknesses discussed. The critical and comparative analysis, which is essential for guiding future research in this rapidly growing field, could be improved.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths of the survey in terms of comprehensiveness, structure, and up-to-date information, coupled with a generally good level of organization and a clear and useful meta-architecture, the paper has merit. However, its limited critical analysis and forward-looking discussion hinder it from being a truly outstanding contribution to the field.
